com.portal.auth;\n\nimport java.util.EnumMap;\nimport java.util.HashMap;\nimport java.util.Map;\n\n/**\n * Failed-attempt limiting, per account <em>and</em> per source, with the decision and the\n * counter mutation performed in one atomic transition.\n *\n * <h2>Why two dimensions</h2>\n * A single account-level counter is a denial-of-service lever: an attacker who knows a\n * colleague's name spends their own handful of guesses to lock that colleague out. So:\n * <ul>\n *   <li><b>per source</b> (validated client address, or \"local\" for the CLI): the tight\n *       budget. It stops the spray and runs out before an attacker can spend much of any\n *       victim's budget.</li>\n *   <li><b>per account</b>: a back-off rather than an indefinite lock. It clears by\n *       itself, grows exponentially while abuse continues, and is cleared by a\n *       <em>correct</em> password, so a locked-out user recovers by authenticating rather\n *       than by waiting for an administrator.</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <h2>Atomicity</h2>\n * {@link #gate}, {@link #recordFailure} and {@link #recordSuccess} each hold one monitor\n * for the whole read-modify-write, then persist the resulting state to the ledger under\n * the same monitor. There is no window in which a concurrent request can observe a\n * count and act on a decision derived from a stale value.\n *\n * <h2>Timing equalisation</h2>\n * Every denial path runs the same KDF work as a wrong-password path\n * ({@link PasswordHasher#verifyAgainstDecoy}) and every refusal is then padded to a\n * common latency floor, so \"you are locked out\" and \"wrong password\" are not\n * distinguishable by response time. See {@link Authenticator#timings()} for the\n * measurement that pins this down.\n */\npublic final class ThrottleManager {\n\n    public static final int ACCOUNT_MAX_FAILURES = 5;\n    public static final int SOURCE_MAX_FAILURES = 30;\n    public static final long WINDOW_SECONDS = 300;\n    /** First back-off after the account budget is spent. */\n    public static final long BASE_BLOCK_SECONDS = 30;\n    /**\n     * Ceiling for an <em>account</em> back-off. Deliberately short (15 minutes, not\n     * hours): a long account lock is a denial-of-service weapon that an attacker can\n     * afford, because they only have to spend five guesses again after it lapses. The\n     * correct password also clears it, so the legitimate user's recovery does not depend\n     * on an administrator.\n     */\n    public static final long MAX_ACCOUNT_BLOCK_SECONDS = 900;\n    /**\n     * Ceiling for a <em>source</em> block. This one is long: the blocked party is the\n     * attacker's own address, so there is no lockout weapon to worry about.\n     */\n    public static final long MAX_SOURCE_BLOCK_SECONDS = 8 * 3600;\n    /** Retain a window this long so a restart cannot outrun it. */\n    public static final long RETENTION_SECONDS = 24 * 3600;\n    private static final int MAX_SUBJECTS = 50_000;\n\n    public enum Kind {\n        /** Allowed to proceed to password verification. */\n        ALLOWED,\n        /** Budget spent: refuse without revealing which dimension tripped. */\n        THROTTLED\n    }\n\n    /** A single subject's counters (account or source). */\n    public static final class State {\n        long windowStart;\n        int failures;\n        long blockedUntil;\n        /** Number of times this subject has tripped the back-off; drives the exponent. */\n        int strikes;\n        /**\n         * Which window {@link #failures} describes. Bumped only where this class\n         * <em>deliberately replaces</em> the window (a success, a rollover, a failure that\n         * arms a back-off), never when a failure is merely added to it. The ledger's merge\n         * needs it to tell \"a sibling counted more attempts in the same window\" from \"the\n         * window was replaced since that sibling wrote\"; without it the on-disk count is\n         * monotonic and cannot be cleared at all -- see {@link Ledger}.\n         */\n        long generation;\n\n        State() {\n            this.windowStart = AuditClock.epochSecond();\n        }\n    }\n\n    private final Object monitor = new Object();\n    private final Map<String, State> accounts = new HashMap<>();\n    private final Map<String, State> sources = new HashMap<>();\n    private final Map<Kind, Long> counters = new EnumMap<>(Kind.class);\n    private final Ledger ledger;\n    private long lastDecisionNanos;\n    private long latencyFloorNanos = 1_000_000L;\n    private boolean paddingSuppressed;\n    private long ledgerLosses;\n\n    public ThrottleManager(Ledger ledger) {\n        this.ledger = ledger;\n        for (Kind k : Kind.values()) {\n            counters.put(k, 0L);\n        }\n        if (ledger != null) {\n            try {\n                loadFromLedger();\n            } catch (RuntimeException ledgerUntrustworthy) {\n                // A corrupt or unreadable ledger is never a reason to allow more\n                // attempts: counters are reset to zero, which is the strictest state,\n                // and the anomaly is reported by the caller through the audit log.\n                accounts.clear();\n                sources.clear();\n                ledgerLosses++;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    public long ledgerLossCount() {\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            return ledgerLosses;\n        }\n    }\n\n    private void loadFromLedger() {\n        long now = AuditClock.epochSecond();\n        Map<String, Ledger.Counter> loaded = ledger.load(now, RETENTION_SECONDS);\n        for (Map.Entry<String, Ledger.Counter> e : loaded.entrySet()) {\n            char kind = e.getKey().charAt(0);\n            String subject = e.getKey().substring(2);\n            State s = new State();\n            s.windowStart = e.getValue().windowStart;\n            s.failures = e.getValue().count;\n            s.blockedUntil = e.getValue().blockedUntil;\n            s.generation = e.getValue().generation;\n            if (s.blockedUntil > s.windowStart) {\n                s.strikes = 1;\n            }\n            (kind == 'a' ? accounts : sources).put(subject, s);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Atomically reserve one verification slot, or refuse.\n     *\n     * <p>This is a <em>reservation</em>, not a peek: the counter is incremented inside the\n     * same monitor that decides the verdict, so a burst of concurrent requests cannot all\n     * read \"4 of 5 failures\" and then all go on to verify. A separate {@code isAllowed()}\n     * followed by a later {@code countFailure()} would be exactly the check-then-act hole\n     * this class exists to close, and it is the reason the verdict and the mutation are one\n     * operation here.\n     *\n     * <p>Cheap and identity-blind by design: it never touches the roster, so the answer\n     * cannot vary between an existing and a non-existing username.\n     *\n     * <p>What \"restriction\" means here, precisely: spending the budget restricts\n     * immediately, and the timed back-off window is armed by {@link #recordFailure} -- the\n     * caller's observation that a reserved verification actually failed. An exhausted\n     * budget with no armed window rolls clean at the next window boundary rather than\n     * blocking for a further period, because the reservation counter alone does not\n     * distinguish five wrong passwords from five requests that were never verified.\n     */\n    public Kind beginAttempt(String accountRef, String sourceHex) {\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            String acct = requireRef(accountRef);\n            long now = AuditClock.epochSecond();\n            State a = live(accounts, acct);\n            State s = live(sources, sourceHex);\n            boolean blocked = a.blockedUntil > now || s.blockedUntil > now\n                    || a.failures >= ACCOUNT_MAX_FAILURES\n                    || s.failures >= SOURCE_MAX_FAILURES;\n            Kind verdict;\n            if (blocked) {\n                verdict = Kind.THROTTLED;\n            } else {\n                a.failures++;\n                s.failures++;\n                verdict = Kind.ALLOWED;\n            }\n            counters.merge(verdict, 1L, Long::sum);\n            lastDecisionNanos = System.nanoTime();\n            persistLocked();\n            return verdict;\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * The reserved slot's verification failed. Applies the back-off if the reserved count\n     * has reached the budget; it does not re-decide anything, and it never re-reads the\n     * caller's expectation.\n     */\n    public void recordFailure(String accountRef, String sourceHex) {\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            String acct = requireRef(accountRef);\n            long now = AuditClock.epochSecond();\n            State a = live(accounts, acct);\n            State s = live(sources, sourceHex);\n            if (a.failures >= ACCOUNT_MAX_FAILURES && a.blockedUntil <= now) {\n                a.strikes = Math.max(1, a.strikes + 1);\n                // The exponent cap must land exactly on MAX_ACCOUNT_BLOCK_SECONDS, not one\n                // step below it. With the old shift cap of 5 the reachable maximum was\n                // 30 << 5 = 480s, so the 900s ceiling stated in this class's documentation\n                // was never enforced and the last doubling was silently truncated. The\n                // shift that first overshoots 900 is 30 << 6 = 1920, so the strike count is\n                // capped at 7 (shift 6) and the min() below is what actually applies the\n                // ceiling -- making the declared limit the enforced one.\n                a.strikes = Math.min(a.strikes, 7);\n                long back = Math.min(MAX_ACCOUNT_BLOCK_SECONDS,\n                        BASE_BLOCK_SECONDS << (a.strikes - 1));\n                a.blockedUntil = now + back;\n                a.windowStart = now;\n                a.failures = 0;\n                a.generation++;\n            }\n            if (s.failures >= SOURCE_MAX_FAILURES && s.blockedUntil <= now) {\n                // Sources get the ceiling immediately: the point of the source budget is to\n                // make an automated spray expensive, not to warn about it. What\n     * keeps that tolerable is the ceiling: the window is short, self-clearing, decays with\n     * a quiet period, and an attacker can only burn one account per\n     * {@code ACCOUNT_MAX_FAILURES} of their own requests before their address is blocked.\n     */\n    public void recordSuccess(String accountRef, String sourceHex) {\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            State a = live(accounts, requireRef(accountRef));\n            a.failures = 0;\n            a.blockedUntil = 0;\n            a.strikes = 0;\n            a.windowStart = AuditClock.epochSecond();\n            a.generation++;\n            counters.merge(Kind.ALLOWED, 1L, Long::sum);\n            persistLocked();\n            lastDecisionNanos = System.nanoTime();\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Consume the source budget for a non-authentication abuse (e.g. forged session id). */\n    public void consumeSourceBudget(String sourceHex) {\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            State s = live(sources, sourceHex);\n            s.failures++;\n            if (s.failures >= SOURCE_MAX_FAILURES) {\n                s.blockedUntil = AuditClock.epochSecond() + MAX_SOURCE_BLOCK_SECONDS;\n                s.windowStart = AuditClock.epochSecond();\n                s.failures = 0;\n                s.generation++;\n            }\n            persistLocked();\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Pad a decision so that every outcome takes about the same wall time. It is only ever a lookup\n        // key, never rendered back to a client. Every shape check lives here, in the one function that produces the answer.\n     */\n    static String[] expandIpv6(String text) {\n        if (text == null || text.isEmpty() || text.length() > 45) {\n            // 45 is the longest legal textual form,\n            // \"0000:0000:0000:0000:0000:0000:255.255.255.255\", so nothing valid is refused\n            // and oversized junk is rejected without being parsed.\n            return null;\n        }\n        String s = text.toLowerCase(java.util.Locale.ROOT);\n        String v4Tail = \"\";\n        int lastColon = s.lastIndexOf(':');\n        String candidateTail = s.substring(lastColon + 1);\n        if (candidateTail.indexOf('.') >= 0) {\n            if (!isLiteralIpv4(candidateTail)) {\n                return null;\n            }\n            v4Tail = candidateTail;\n            s = s.substring(0, lastColon + 1) + v4ToTailGroups(candidateTail);\n        }\n        String left;\n        String right;\n        int dc = s.indexOf(\"::\");\n        if (dc >= 0) {\n            left = s.substring(0, dc);\n            right = s.substring(dc + 2);\n        } else {\n            left = s;\n            right = null;\n        }\n        List<String> low = groupsOf(left, true);\n        if (low == null) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        List<String> high = new ArrayList<>();\n        if (right != null) {\n            high = groupsOf(right, false);\n            if (high == null) {\n                return null;\n            }\n            if (low.size() + high.size() > 7) {\n                return null;\n            }\n        } else if (low.size() != 8) {\n            return null;\n        } else {\n            high = List.of();\n        }\n        List<String> out = new ArrayList<>(low);\n        while (out.size() < 8 - We do not \"fix\"\n *       somebody else's file, because we cannot know who read it while it was open.</li>\n *   <li>Reads are size-bounded before the bytes are turned into characters.</li>\n * </ul>\n */\npublic final class PrivateFiles {\n\n    public static final Set<PosixFilePermission> FILE_MODE =\n            PosixFilePermissions.fromString(\"rw-------\");\n    public static final Set<PosixFilePermission> DIR_MODE =\n            PosixFilePermissions.fromString(\"rwx------\");\n\n    private PrivateFiles() {\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Prepares (or accepts) the data directory. }\n\n    public static UserPrincipal owner(Path p) {\n        try {\n            return Files.getOwner(p, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS);\n        } catch (IOException e) {\n            throw new A credential posted over cleartext is a leaked credential,\n *       so \"we also serve plain HTTP for convenience\" is not an available configuration.\n *       </li>\n *   <li><b>Method allowlist, pinned content type, no chunked bodies.</b> Anything else is a\n *       405/415 whose shape does not depend on the caller's credentials.</li>\n *   <li><b>Hard body cap.</b> Read in bounded chunks and abandoned the moment it is\n *       exceeded, so a 10 GB POST costs us 8 KiB.</li>\n *   <li><b>Form keys are an allowlist</b> ({@code username}, {@code password},\n *       {@code csrf}); an unknown key or a repeated key refuses the whole request. Extra\n *       keys are how mass-assignment gets into an authentication form, and a repeated key\n *       lets the client pick which of two values the server sees.</li>\n *   <li><b>CSRF token pair required on every state-changing request.</b> Both halves must\n *       be present, well-formed, equal to each other and match a pair we issued; the pair\n *       is consumed either way so it cannot be replayed.</li>\n *   <li><b>One response for every authentication denial.</b> Unknown user, wrong password,\n *       malformed input, spent budget, bad CSRF, missing or oversized body, oversized\n *       header: same status, same bytes, same header names and values, same\n *       {@code Content-Length}. The distinction exists only in the audit log.</li>\n *   <li><b>Cookies are {@code HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Strict; Path=/}.</b> The session\n *       cookie is minted only after a verified password match and is never accepted as\n *       anything but a lookup key.</li>\n *   <li><b>Source attribution.</b> The socket peer decides the per-source budget.\n *       {@code X-Forwarded-For} is ignored unless the peer is inside the configured trusted\n *       proxy set, and then only by walking right-to-left (see\n *       {@link SourceRef#resolvePeer}).</li>\n * </ol>\n *\n * <h2>A known, accepted shape difference</h2>\n * {@code HEAD} cannot carry a body, and the JDK server omits {@code Content-Length} on\n * {@code HEAD} responses. route(HttpExchangeView ex) throws IOException {\n        String method = ex.method() == null ? \"\" : ex.method();\n        String path = ex.rawPath() == null ? \"\" : ex.rawPath();\n\n        if (!method.equals(\"GET\") && !method.equals(\"POST\")) {\n            ex.respond(405, HttpExchangeView.EMPTY, securityHeaders());\n            return;\n        }\n        if (method.equals(\"GET\")) }\n        if (!hasFormContentType(ex)) {\n            ex.respond(415, HttpExchangeView.EMPTY, securityHeaders());\n            return;\n        }\n        byte[] body = readBody(ex);\n        if does not depend on it. */\n    public void purgeExpiredSessions() {\n        runtime.sessions().purgeExpired();\n    }\n\n    // ---------------------------------------------------------------- login runtime.sessions().issueCsrfToken(token, token);\n\n        StringBuilder html = new StringBuilder(1024);\n        html.append(\"<!doctype html><html lang=\\\"en\\\"><head><meta charset=\\\"utf-8\\\">\")\n                .append(\"<meta name=\\\"viewport\\\" content=\\\"width=device-width\\\">\")\n                .append(\"<title>Employee portal sign in</title></head><body>\")\n                .append(\"<h1>Employee portal</h1>\")\n                .append(\"<form method=\\\"post\\\" action=\\\"/login\\\" autocomplete=\\\"off\\\">\")\n                .append(\"<label for=\\\"u\\\">Username</label>\")\n                .append(\"<input id=\\\"u\\\" name=\\\"username\\\" maxlength=\\\"\")\n                .append(InputPolicy.USERNAME_MAX).append(\"\\\" required>\")\n                .append(\"<label for=\\\"p\\\">Password</label>\")\n                .append(\"<input id=\\\"p\\\" name=\\\"password\\\" type=\\\"password\\\" maxlength=\\\"\")\n                .append(InputPolicy.PASSWORD_MAX).append(\"\\\" required>\")\n                .append(\"<input type=\\\"hidden\\\" name=\\\"csrf\\\" value=\\\"\").append(htmlEscape(token))\n                .append(\"\\\"><button type=\\\"submit\\\">Sign in</button></form>\")\n                .append(\"</body></html>\");\n        byte[] page = html.toString().getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n\n        List<String> headers = securityHeaders();\n        headers.add(csrfCookie(token));\n        ex.respond(200, page, headers);\n    }\n\n    // ------------------------------------------------------------------- actions\n\n    private void login(HttpExchangeView ex, FormFields fields) throws IOException {\n        // The label, not the reference: authenticate() performs the one reduction this\n        // request gets, and sharing a bucket with the CSRF-abuse counter depends on it.\n        String sourceLabel = sourceLabelOf(ex);\n\n        byte[] userBytes = fields.asciiBytes(FormFields.USERNAME);\n        byte[] passBytes = fields.asciiBytes(FormFields.PASSWORD);\n        if (userBytes == null || passBytes == null) {\n            Crypto.wipe(passBytes);\n            // A form with no credential field is not an authentication attempt, so it is\n            // charged to the source directly rather than through the Authenticator -- but\n            // into the same bucket the Authenticator would have used.\n            runtime.throttle().consumeSourceBudget(SourceRef.normalizeForLedger(sourceLabel));\n            denied(ex);\n            return;\n        }\n        String username = new String(userBytes, StandardCharsets.US_ASCII);\n        Crypto.wipe(userBytes);\n        char[] password = charsFromBytes(passBytes);\n        Crypto.wipe(passBytes);\n\n        Authenticator.Result result =\n                runtime.authenticator().authenticate(username, password, sourceLabel);\n        // authenticate() clears the array on every path; this reference is now inert.\n        if (!result.isSuccess()) {\n            denied(ex);\n            return;\n        }\n        List<String> headers = securityHeaders();\n        headers.add(sessionCookie(result.sessionId()));\n        headers.add(clearedCsrfCookie());\n        headers.add(\"Location: /login\");\n        ex.respond(303, HttpExchangeView.EMPTY, headers);\n    }\n\n    private void logout(HttpExchangeView ex) throws IOException {\n        Optional<String> presented = CookieParser.value(ex.header(\"Cookie\"), SESSION_COOKIE);\n        if (presented.isEmpty() || runtime.sessions().find(presented.get()).isEmpty()) {\n            // Nothing to revoke, and the answer is the same generic denial every\n            // unauthenticated request gets. A session-id probe cannot tell \"expired\" from\n            // \"never existed\" from \"made up\".\n            denied(ex);\n            return;\n        }\n        runtime.sessions().logout(presented.get());\n        List<String> headers = securityHeaders();\n        headers.add(clearedSessionCookie());\n        headers.add(clearedCsrfCookie());\n        ex.respond(200, SIGNED_OUT_BODY.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8), headers);\n    }\n\n    // ------------------------------------------------------------------ responses\n\n    /** The single denial response. Every caller of this produces byte-identical output. */\n    private void denied(HttpExchangeView ex) throws IOException {\n        ex.respond(401, DENIED_BODY.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8), securityHeaders());\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * The response headers every reply carries, so a denial cannot be distinguished from\n     * another denial by a header that only one branch remembered to set.\n     *\n     * <p>There is deliberately no variant flag. An earlier version omitted\n     * {@code Strict-Transport-Security} on the 404/405/415/500 replies and sent it on the\n     * authentication replies, which contradicted this comment and made the header block\n     * itself differ by branch -- the exact shape difference this method exists to prevent.\n     * Sending it everywhere is also the only useful choice: the first response a browser\n     * gets from a host is often a 404 on a stale bookmark, and a policy published only on\n     * pages the user has not reached yet protects nothing.\n     *\n     * <p>{@code no-store} is not decoration: a cached 401 or a cached login page in a shared\n     * browser is a credential and CSRF-token leak.\n     */\n    private List<String> securityHeaders() {\n        List<String> h = new ArrayList<>(10);\n        h.add(\"Cache-Control: no-store\");\n        h.add(\"Pragma: no-cache\");\n        h.add(\"X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff\");\n        h.add(\"X-Frame-Options: DENY\");\n        h.add(\"Referrer-Policy: no-referrer\");\n        h.add(\"Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; script-src 'none'; style-src 'self';\"\n                + \" img-src 'self'; form-action 'self'; base-uri 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'\");\n        h.add(\"Vary: Cookie\");\n        h.add(\"Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=\" + config.hstsMaxAgeSeconds());\n        return h;\n    }\n\n    // ------------------------------------------------------------------- cookies\n\n    /**\n     * {@code HttpOnly} on both cookies. The login form's CSRF value is rendered into the\n     * page as a hidden field, so nothing needs script access, and keeping the cookie\n     * unreadable to script means injected script cannot plant a matching pair.\n     */\n    private String sessionCookie(String id) {\n        return \"Set-Cookie: \" + SESSION_COOKIE + \"=\" + id\n                + \"; Path=/; HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Strict; Max-Age=\"\n                + SessionManager.ABSOLUTE_TIMEOUT.getSeconds();\n    }\n\n    private String clearedSessionCookie() {\n        return \"Set-Cookie: \" + SESSION_COOKIE + \"=; Path=/; HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Strict;\"\n                + \" Max-Age=0\";\n    }\n\n    private String csrfCookie(String token) {\n        return \"Set-Cookie: \" + CSRF_COOKIE + \"=\" + token\n                + \"; Path=/; HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Strict; Max-Age=\"\n                + SessionManager.CSRF_TIMEOUT.getSeconds();\n    }\n\n    private String clearedCsrfCookie() {\n        return \"Set-Cookie: \" + CSRF_COOKIE + \"=; Path=/; HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Strict;\"\n                + \" Max-Age=0\";\n    }\n\n    // -------------------------------------------------------------------- bodies\n\n    /** @return the body, or null when it is missing, empty or over the cap. */\n    private byte[] readBody(HttpExchangeView ex) throws IOException {\n        byte[] raw = ex.readBodyBounded(MAX_BODY_BYTES);\n        if (raw == null || raw.length == 0 || raw.length > MAX_BODY_BYTES) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        return raw;\n    }\n\n    private boolean hasFormContentType(HttpExchangeView ex) {\n        String ct = ex.header(\"Content-Type\");\n        if (ct == null || ct.length() > 200) {\n            return false;\n        }\n        int semi = ct.indexOf(';');\n        String media = semi >= 0 ? ct.substring(0, semi) : ct;\n        return media.equalsIgnoreCase(\"application/x-www-form-urlencoded\");\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Consumes the CSRF pair. Charged to the source budget on failure: a real browser\n     * never fails this check, so a failure is an automated client.\n     */\n    private boolean csrfAcceptable(HttpExchangeView ex, FormFields fields) {\n        String cookie = CookieParser.value(ex.header(\"Cookie\"), CSRF_COOKIE).orElse(null);\n        byte[] hidden = fields.asciiBytes(FormFields.CSRF);\n        if (hidden == null) {\n            return false;\n        }\n        String hiddenValue = new String(hidden, StandardCharsets.US_ASCII);\n        Crypto.wipe(hidden);\n        return runtime.sessions().consumeCsrfToken(cookie, hiddenValue);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * The resolved client <em>label</em> for this request: a literal address, or\n     * {@code \"unknown\"}. Not a ledger key -- see {@link #sourceHexOf}.\n     */\n    private String sourceLabelOf(HttpExchangeView ex) {\n        return SourceRef.resolvePeer(ex.peerAddress(), ex.header(\"X-Forwarded-For\"),\n                trustedProxies);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * The ledger and audit key for this request.\n     *\n     * <p>The reduction from label to reference happens <b>exactly once</b> per request, and\n     * which call site owns it matters: {@link Authenticator} reduces whatever label it is\n     * handed, so this class must pass it the label rather than an already-reduced reference.\n     * Doing it twice is not an error that announces itself -- {@code ref(ref(x))} is a valid\n     * key, just a <em>different</em> one -- so the symptom was that CSRF abuse and password\n     * failures for one address were counted in two separate buckets, each of which throttled\n     * on its own. A value that decodes to anything outside printable ASCII is\n     * refused rather than substituted, so this boundary never invents a credential.\n     */\n    static final class FormFields {\n\n        static final String USERNAME = \"username\";\n        static final String PASSWORD = \"password\";\n        static final String CSRF = \"csrf\";\n        private static final List<String> ALLOWED = List.of(USERNAME, PASSWORD, CSRF);\n\n        private final java.util.Map<String, byte[]> values = new java.util.HashMap<>();\n\n        /**\n         * Requires the syntax, the allowlist and the CSRF field -- the one field every\n         * route needs.\n         *\n         * <p>It deliberately does <em>not</em> require {@code username} and\n         * {@code password}: this parser is shared with {@code POST /logout}, which carries\n         * only the CSRF pair, so requiring the credential fields here made the logout\n         * endpoint unreachable -- every sign-out attempt was refused as a malformed form and\n         * the only way a session ended was to wait for it to expire, which contradicts what\n         * {@link SessionManager} promises about revocation. Which fields a route requires is\n         * the route's decision: {@link #login} refuses when a credential field is absent.\n         */\n        static FormFields parse(String body) {\n            if (body.isEmpty() || body.length() > MAX_BODY_BYTES) {\n                return null;\n            }\n            FormFields out = new FormFields();\n            for (String pair : body.split(\"&\")) {\n                if (pair.isEmpty()) {\n                    return null;\n                }\n                int eq = pair.indexOf('=');\n                if (eq <= 0 || eq == pair.length() - 1) {\n                    return null;\n                }\n                String key = percentDecode(pair.substring(0, eq), true);\n                if (key == null || !ALLOWED.contains(key) || out.values.containsKey(key)) {\n                    return null;\n                }\n                byte[] value = percentDecodeBytes(pair.substring(eq + 1));\n                if (value == null || value.length == 0 || value.length > MAX_FIELD) {\n                    return null;\n                }\n                out.values.put(key, value);\n            }\n            if (!out.values.containsKey(CSRF)) {\n                return null;\n            }\n            return out;\n        }\n\n        byte[] asciiBytes(String key) {\n            byte[] raw = values.get(key);\n            return raw == null ? null : Arrays.copyOf(raw, raw.length);\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static String percentDecode(String s, boolean plusIsSpace) {\n        byte[] d = percentDecodeBytes(s);\n        if (d == null) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        String decoded = new String(d, StandardCharsets.US_ASCII);\n        Crypto.wipe(d);\n        if (plusIsSpace) {\n            decoded = decoded.replace('+', ' ');\n        }\n        return decoded;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Decodes percent-escapes, requiring every resulting byte to be printable ASCII\n     * ({@code 0x21..0x7E}). Anything else -- a control byte, a space escape, a non-ASCII\n     * UTF-8 sequence, a truncated escape -- returns null.\n     */\n    private static byte[] percentDecodeBytes(String s) {\n        if (s.length() > MAX_FIELD * 3 + 8) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        ByteArrayOutputStream out = new ByteArrayOutputStream(s.length());\n        for (int i = 0; i < s.length(); i++) {\n            char c = s.charAt(i);\n            if (c == '%') {\n                if (i + 2 >= s.length()) {\n                    return null;\n                }\n                int hi = Character.digit(s.charAt(i + 1), 16);\n                int lo = Character.digit(s.charAt(i + 2), 16);\n                if (hi < 0 || lo < 0) {\n                    return null;\n                }\n                int v = (hi << 4) | lo;\n                if (v < 0x21 || v > 0x7E) {\n                    return null;\n                }\n                out.write(v);\n                i += 2;\n                continue;\n            }\n            if (c < 0x21 || c > 0x7E) {\n                return null;\n            }\n            out.write(c);\n        }\n        return out.toByteArray();\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"5cf4ac4e-dfb3-46e4-9949-f08936267348","session_id":"43a3d1fe-7aa6-43e7-b332-fdd94a07da51","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"5cf4ac4e-dfb3-46e4-9949-f08936267348","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Note in `route`: CSRF failure path calls `sourceHexOf(ex)` which reduces twice — but that's for consumeSourceBudget, which is a different bucket than the Authenticator's. listener: the JDK's built-in {@link HttpsServer}, configured rather than\n * reimplemented.\n *\n * <p>Platform-preference notes (rule 8):\n * <ul>\n *   <li>TLS comes from {@code javax.net.ssl} with the JDK's own trust defaults. No\n *       third-party HTTP or TLS library is involved, so there is nothing to pin, verify or\n *       patch beyond the JDK itself (see {@code tools/get-jdk.sh}, which checks Adoptium's\n *       published SHA-256 for the JDK this is built with).</li>\n *   <li>Protocols and cipher suites are the JDK's current defaults with TLSv1 and TLSv1.1\n *       removed explicitly -- an allowlist by removal, which is safer than a hand-written\n *       suite list that quietly pins tomorrow's weak cipher.</li>\n *   <li>The request/response header and time limits use the server's own documented system\n *       properties rather than a bespoke parser.</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <p>The keystore password is read from a private file named by the environment. It is\n * never a command-line argument (world-readable through {@code /proc}), never printed, and\n * the {@code char[]} it lands in is cleared as soon as the {@code SSLContext} is built.\n */\npublic final class JdkHttpsListener implements AutoCloseable {\n\n    private final HttpsServer server;\n    private final ScheduledExecutorService janitor;\n\n    private JdkHttpsListener(HttpsServer server, ScheduledExecutorService janitor) {\n        this.server = server;\n        this.janitor = janitor;\n    }\n\n    public static JdkHttpsListener start(PortalConfig config, PortalServer handler)\n            throws IOException {\n        if (config.keystorePath() == null || config.keystorePasswordFile() == null) {\n            // No HTTP fallback, by design. Refusing to serve is the only alternative to\n            // transmitting credentials in the clear.\n            throw new IllegalStateException(\"https-keystore-not-configured\");\n        }\n        // Bounded header counts and per-request time limits, via the server's own knobs.\n        setIfAbsent(\"sun.net.httpserver.maxReqHeaders\", \"64\");\n        setIfAbsent(\"sun.net.httpserver.maxRspHeaders\", \"64\");\n        setIfAbsent(\"sun.net.httpserver.maxReqTime\", \"30\");\n        setIfAbsent(\"sun.net.httpserver.maxRspTime\", \"30\");\n\n        SSLContext sslContext = buildSslContext(config.keystorePath(),\n                config.keystorePasswordFile());\n\n        InetSocketAddress bind = new InetSocketAddress(config.listenHost(), config.listenPort());\n        HttpsServer server = HttpsServer.create(bind, 64);\n        server.setHttpsConfigurator(new HttpsConfigurator(sslContext) {\n            @Override\n            public void configure(HttpsParameters params) {\n                javax.net.ssl.SSLEngine engine = sslContext.createSSLEngine();\n                List<String> protocols = new ArrayList<>();\n                for (String p : engine.getSupportedProtocols()) {\n                    if (p.equals(\"TLSv1.2\") || p.equals(\"TLSv1.3\")) {\n                        protocols.add(p);\n                    }\n                }\n                if (protocols.isEmpty()) {\n                    throw new IllegalStateException(\"no-acceptable-tls-protocol\");\n                }\n                params.setProtocols(protocols.toArray(new String[0]));\n                params.setCipherSuites(engine.getEnabledCipherSuites());\n                SSLParameters base = sslContext.getDefaultSSLParameters();\n                params.setNeedClientAuth(base.getNeedClientAuth());\n            }\n        });\n        // Bounded pool: an unbounded cached pool turns a connection flood into an OOM. An\n *       unknown subcommand or flag is a refusal, never a fallback. <b>No secret may be\n *       passed on argv</b> -- command lines are readable by any user through\n *       {@code /proc/<pid>/cmdline} -- so {@code add-user} takes no password argument and\n *       reads it from the terminal instead.</li>\n *   <li><b>stdin</b>: the username line, and the password when there is no controlling\n *       terminal (batch mode). addUser(false);\n                case \"disable-user\" -> disableUser(argv);\n                case \"status\" -> status();\n                default -> {\n                    out.println(\"unknown Fail closed: no command runs, and the operator gets\n            // a machine token, not the message -- which can name a path.\n            out.println(\"operation-refused reason=\" + It is\n     * never taken from an argument, where every other user could read it.\n     */\n    private char[] readSecretChars(String prompt) throws IOException {\n        if (haveConsole) {\n            char[] secret = console.readPassword(prompt);\n            if (secret == null) {\n                return null;\n            }\n            // readPassword strips only the line terminator.\n            return secret;\n        }\n        out.print(prompt);\n        out.flush();\n        return readRawLine();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Bounded line read. {@code BufferedReader.readLine} allocates for a line of any\n     * length, so a multi-gigabyte stdin would be a memory-exhaustion DoS against a program\n     * whose own policy refuses anything longer than {@link InputPolicy#LINE_MAX}. One\n     * character past the cap and the remainder of the line is drained and discarded.\n     *\n     * <p>On overflow we return an array that is deliberately too long to be a valid\n     * credential, so the caller's own boundary check produces the generic refusal. We do\n     * not truncate: a truncated password is a different secret from the one typed, and a\n     * truncated username could land on somebody else's real account.\n     *\n     * <h2>Carriage returns terminate a line, they are never deleted</h2>\n     * An earlier draft wrote {@code if (c == '\\r') continue;} to accept CRLF input, which\n     * silently deleted every CR in the <em>middle</em> of a line as well. Measured against\n     * the shipped binary that folded {@code \"ali\\rce\"} onto the real account {@code alice}\n     * and made {@code \"Fresh-Quiver-7\\r1!\"} verify as the real password -- both granted with\n     * exit code {@code 0}. Deleting an invisible character from a credential is exactly the\n     * identity folding {@link InputPolicy} refuses to do with {@code trim()}; it was\n     * happening in the one reader that policy never sees.\n     *\n     * <p>CR is therefore a line terminator with {@code BufferedReader.readLine} semantics:\n     * {@code LF}, {@code CRLF} or a lone {@code CR} ends the line, and the character after a\n     * lone {@code CR} begins the next one. A terminator can only <em>split</em> a credential,\n     * and a split is always safe here -- each half is a different string from any real\n     * credential, so the outcome is a refusal rather than a login as somebody else. Keeping\n     * CR as content instead would be equally safe but would reject a legitimate CRLF batch\n     * file, which pushes operators to pre-strip input by hand -- a workaround that eventually\n     * strips something that mattered.\n     */\n    private char[] readRawLine() throws IOException {\n        char[] buf = new char[InputPolicy.LINE_MAX];\n        int n = 0;\n        boolean overflowed = false;\n        int c = -1;\n        while ((c = in.read()) >= 0) {\n            if (c == '\\n') {\n                break;\n            }\n            if (c == '\\r') {\n                int after = in.read();\n                if (after >= 0 && after != '\\n') {\n                    in.unread(after);\n                }\n                break;\n            }\n            if (n < buf.length) {\n                buf[n++] = (char) c;\n            } else {\n                overflowed = true;\n            }\n        }\n        if (c < 0 && n == 0) {\n            Crypto.wipe(buf);\n            return null;\n        }\n        if (overflowed) {\n            Crypto.wipe(buf);\n            return new char[InputPolicy.PASSWORD_MAX + 1];\n        }\n        char[] exact = new char[n];\n        System.arraycopy(buf, 0, exact, 0, n);\n        Crypto.wipe(buf);\n        return exact;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Reduces an internal exception message to a printable machine token. So:\n * <ul>\n *   <li><b>per source</b> (validated client address, or \"local\" for the CLI): the tight\n *       budget. It stops the spray and runs out before an attacker can spend much of any\n *       victim's budget.</li>\n *   <li><b>per account</b>: a back-off rather than an indefinite lock. It clears by\n *       itself, grows exponentially while abuse continues, and is cleared by a\n *       <em>correct</em> password, so a locked-out user recovers by authenticating rather\n *       than by waiting for an administrator.</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <h2>Atomicity</h2>\n * {@link #gate}, {@link #recordFailure} and {@link #recordSuccess} each hold one monitor\n * for the whole read-modify-write, then persist the resulting state to the ledger under\n * the same monitor. There is no window in which a concurrent request can observe a\n * count and act on a decision derived from a stale value.\n *\n * <h2>Timing equalisation</h2>\n * Every denial path runs the same KDF work as a wrong-password path\n * ({@link PasswordHasher#verifyAgainstDecoy}) and every refusal is then padded to a\n * common latency floor, so \"you are locked out\" and \"wrong password\" are not\n * distinguishable by response time. See {@link Authenticator#timings()} for the\n * measurement that pins this down.\n */\npublic final class ThrottleManager {\n\n    public static final int ACCOUNT_MAX_FAILURES = 5;\n    public static final int SOURCE_MAX_FAILURES = 30;\n    public static final long WINDOW_SECONDS = 300;\n    /** First back-off after the account budget is spent. */\n    public static final long BASE_BLOCK_SECONDS = 30;\n    /**\n     * Ceiling for an <em>account</em> back-off. Deliberately short (15 minutes, not\n     * hours): a long account lock is a denial-of-service weapon that an attacker can\n     * afford, because they only have to spend five guesses again after it lapses. The\n     * correct password also clears it, so the legitimate user's recovery does not depend\n     * on an administrator.\n     */\n    public static final long MAX_ACCOUNT_BLOCK_SECONDS = 900;\n    /**\n     * Ceiling for a <em>source</em> block. This one is long: the blocked party is the\n     * attacker's own address, so there is no lockout weapon to worry about.\n     */\n    public static final long MAX_SOURCE_BLOCK_SECONDS = 8 * 3600;\n    /** Retain a window this long so a restart cannot outrun it. */\n    public static final long RETENTION_SECONDS = 24 * 3600;\n    private static final int MAX_SUBJECTS = 50_000;\n\n    public enum Kind {\n        /** Allowed to proceed to password verification. */\n        ALLOWED,\n        /** Budget spent: refuse without revealing which dimension tripped. */\n        THROTTLED\n    }\n\n    /** A single subject's counters (account or source). */\n    public static final class State {\n        long windowStart;\n        int failures;\n        long blockedUntil;\n        /** Number of times this subject has tripped the back-off; drives the exponent. */\n        int strikes;\n        /**\n         * Which window {@link #failures} describes. Bumped only where this class\n         * <em>deliberately replaces</em> the window (a success, a rollover, a failure that\n         * arms a back-off), never when a failure is merely added to it. The ledger's merge\n         * needs it to tell \"a sibling counted more attempts in the same window\" from \"the\n         * window was replaced since that sibling wrote\"; without it the on-disk count is\n         * monotonic and cannot be cleared at all -- see {@link Ledger}.\n         */\n        long generation;\n\n        State() {\n            this.windowStart = AuditClock.epochSecond();\n        }\n    }\n\n    private final Object monitor = new Object();\n    private final Map<String, State> accounts = new HashMap<>();\n    private final Map<String, State> sources = new HashMap<>();\n    private final Map<Kind, Long> counters = new EnumMap<>(Kind.class);\n    private final Ledger ledger;\n    private long lastDecisionNanos;\n    private long latencyFloorNanos = 1_000_000L;\n    private boolean paddingSuppressed;\n    private long ledgerLosses;\n\n    public ThrottleManager(Ledger ledger) {\n        this.ledger = ledger;\n        for (Kind k : Kind.values()) {\n            counters.put(k, 0L);\n        }\n        if (ledger != null) {\n            try {\n                loadFromLedger();\n            } catch (RuntimeException ledgerUntrustworthy) {\n                // A corrupt or unreadable ledger is never a reason to allow more\n                // attempts: counters are reset to zero, which is the strictest state,\n                // and the anomaly is reported by the caller through the audit log.\n                accounts.clear();\n                sources.clear();\n                ledgerLosses++;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    public long ledgerLossCount() {\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            return ledgerLosses;\n        }\n    }\n\n    private void loadFromLedger() {\n        long now = AuditClock.epochSecond();\n        Map<String, Ledger.Counter> loaded = ledger.load(now, RETENTION_SECONDS);\n        for (Map.Entry<String, Ledger.Counter> e : loaded.entrySet()) {\n            char kind = e.getKey().charAt(0);\n            String subject = e.getKey().substring(2);\n            State s = new State();\n            s.windowStart = e.getValue().windowStart;\n            s.failures = e.getValue().count;\n            s.blockedUntil = e.getValue().blockedUntil;\n            s.generation = e.getValue().generation;\n            if (s.blockedUntil > s.windowStart) {\n                s.strikes = 1;\n            }\n            (kind == 'a' ? accounts : sources).put(subject, s);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Atomically reserve one verification slot, or refuse.\n     *\n     * <p>This is a <em>reservation</em>, not a peek: the counter is incremented inside the\n     * same monitor that decides the verdict, so a burst of concurrent requests cannot all\n     * read \"4 of 5 failures\" and then all go on to verify. A separate {@code isAllowed()}\n     * followed by a later {@code countFailure()} would be exactly the check-then-act hole\n     * this class exists to close, and it is the reason the verdict and the mutation are one\n     * operation here.\n     *\n     * <p>Cheap and identity-blind by design: it never touches the roster, so the answer\n     * cannot vary between an existing and a non-existing username.\n     *\n     * <p>What \"restriction\" means here, precisely: spending the budget restricts\n     * immediately, and the timed back-off window is armed by {@link #recordFailure} -- the\n     * caller's observation that a reserved verification actually failed. An exhausted\n     * budget with no armed window rolls clean at the next window boundary rather than\n     * blocking for a further period, because the reservation counter alone does not\n     * distinguish five wrong passwords from five requests that were never verified.\n     */\n    public Kind beginAttempt(String accountRef, String sourceHex) {\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            String acct = requireRef(accountRef);\n            long now = AuditClock.epochSecond();\n            State a = live(accounts, acct);\n            State s = live(sources, sourceHex);\n            boolean blocked = a.blockedUntil > now || s.blockedUntil > now\n                    || a.failures >= ACCOUNT_MAX_FAILURES\n                    || s.failures >= SOURCE_MAX_FAILURES;\n            Kind verdict;\n            if (blocked) {\n                verdict = Kind.THROTTLED;\n            } else {\n                a.failures++;\n                s.failures++;\n                verdict = Kind.ALLOWED;\n            }\n            counters.merge(verdict, 1L, Long::sum);\n            lastDecisionNanos = System.nanoTime();\n            persistLocked();\n            return verdict;\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * The reserved slot's verification failed. Applies the back-off if the reserved count\n     * has reached the budget; it does not re-decide anything, and it never re-reads the\n     * caller's expectation.\n     */\n    public void recordFailure(String accountRef, String sourceHex) {\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            String acct = requireRef(accountRef);\n            long now = AuditClock.epochSecond();\n            State a = live(accounts, acct);\n            State s = live(sources, sourceHex);\n            if (a.failures >= ACCOUNT_MAX_FAILURES && a.blockedUntil <= now) {\n                a.strikes = Math.max(1, a.strikes + 1);\n                // The exponent cap must land exactly on MAX_ACCOUNT_BLOCK_SECONDS, not one\n                // step below it. With the old shift cap of 5 the reachable maximum was\n                // 30 << 5 = 480s, so the 900s ceiling stated in this class's documentation\n                // was never enforced and the last doubling was silently truncated. The\n                // shift that first overshoots 900 is 30 << 6 = 1920, so the strike count is\n                // capped at 7 (shift 6) and the min() below is what actually applies the\n                // ceiling -- making the declared limit the enforced one.\n                a.strikes = Math.min(a.strikes, 7);\n                long back = Math.min(MAX_ACCOUNT_BLOCK_SECONDS,\n                        BASE_BLOCK_SECONDS << (a.strikes - 1));\n                a.blockedUntil = now + back;\n                a.windowStart = now;\n                a.failures = 0;\n                a.generation++;\n            }\n            if (s.failures >= SOURCE_MAX_FAILURES && s.blockedUntil <= now) {\n                // Sources get the ceiling immediately: the point of the source budget is to\n                // make an automated spray expensive, not to warn about it. The blocked\n                // party here is the attacker's own address, so a long ceiling is not a\n                // lockout weapon -- see MAX_ACCOUNT_BLOCK_SECONDS for why the account\n                // ceiling is short instead.\n                s.blockedUntil = now + MAX_SOURCE_BLOCK_SECONDS;\n                s.windowStart = now;\n                s.failures = 0;\n                s.generation++;\n            }\n            persistLocked();\n            lastDecisionNanos = System.nanoTime();\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * A correct password clears the account's counters, so partial harassment (a few\n     * failures burned before the real user signs in) never accumulates into a lockout.\n     *\n     * <p>Stated precisely, because the first version of this comment overclaimed: this\n     * method clears a window that is <em>already running</em> only if\n     * {@link #beginAttempt} handed out a slot, which it does not do while the account is\n     * blocked. So the recovery path for a user whose budget was burned by an attacker is\n     * that the window lapses on its own, not that a correct password overrides it. What\n     * keeps that tolerable is the ceiling: the window is short, self-clearing, decays with\n     * a quiet period, and an attacker can only burn one account per\n     * {@code ACCOUNT_MAX_FAILURES} of their own requests before their address is blocked.\n     */\n    public void recordSuccess(String accountRef, String sourceHex) {\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            State a = live(accounts, requireRef(accountRef));\n            a.failures = 0;\n            a.blockedUntil = 0;\n            a.strikes = 0;\n            a.windowStart = AuditClock.epochSecond();\n            a.generation++;\n            counters.merge(Kind.ALLOWED, 1L, Long::sum);\n            persistLocked();\n            lastDecisionNanos = System.nanoTime();\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Consume the source budget for a non-authentication abuse (e.g. forged session id). */\n    public void consumeSourceBudget(String sourceHex) {\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            State s = live(sources, sourceHex);\n            s.failures++;\n            if (s.failures >= SOURCE_MAX_FAILURES) {\n                s.blockedUntil = AuditClock.epochSecond() + MAX_SOURCE_BLOCK_SECONDS;\n                s.windowStart = AuditClock.epochSecond();\n                s.failures = 0;\n                s.generation++;\n            }\n            persistLocked();\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Pad a decision so that every outcome takes about the same wall time. Must be called\n     * outside the monitor: it burns CPU on purpose.\n     *\n     * <p>{@code startedAt} is the caller's request-start timestamp rather than a shared\n     * field: under concurrency a shared \"last decision\" value belongs to whichever request\n     * happened to write it last, which would make the padding arbitrary.\n     *\n     * <p>This performs no key derivation. The KDF work that every path must share is done\n     * by {@link Authenticator}, exactly once per request -- doing it here as well would\n     * make the per-path work differ, which is precisely the channel this padding exists\n     * to close.\n     */\n    public void padToFloor(long startedAtNanos) {\n        long floor;\n        if (paddingSuppressed) {\n            return;\n        }\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            floor = latencyFloorNanos;\n        }\n        long elapsed = System.nanoTime() - startedAtNanos;\n        long remaining = floor - elapsed;\n        if (remaining > 0) {\n            long deadline = System.nanoTime() + remaining;\n            // Busy-wait rather than sleep: Thread.sleep granularity is ~1 ms on this box\n            // and the point of the padding is sub-millisecond uniformity.\n            while (System.nanoTime() < deadline) {\n                Thread.onSpinWait();\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Observe the slowest refusal so far and use it as the common floor. */\n    public void observeLatency(long nanos) {\n        if (paddingSuppressed) {\n            return;\n        }\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            if (nanos > latencyFloorNanos) {\n                latencyFloorNanos = nanos;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Test controls for the timing assertions.\n     *\n     * <p>{@code suppressPadding} makes both the padding <b>and</b> the floor ratchet no-ops,\n     * so a measurement sees the paths' real cost. It has to disable both: if the ratchet\n     * stayed live, the first measured path would raise the floor and every later path would\n     * be padded up to it, so a mutant that deletes a derivation from one branch would still\n     * show a perfect spread.\n     *\n     * <p>{@code raiseFloorNanos} only ever <em>raises</em> the floor, so it cannot loosen\n     * production behaviour. With the floor set well above the KDF cost, every outcome must\n     * take at least the floor -- an assertion the padding actually has to satisfy, which a\n     * spread-only assertion would never catch.\n     */\n    void setTimingControlsForTesting(boolean suppressPadding, long raiseFloorNanos) {\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            this.paddingSuppressed = suppressPadding;\n            if (raiseFloorNanos > this.latencyFloorNanos) {\n                this.latencyFloorNanos = raiseFloorNanos;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    public long latencyFloorNanos() {\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            return latencyFloorNanos;\n        }\n    }\n\n    public long countOf(Kind kind) {\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            return counters.getOrDefault(kind, 0L);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Test/inspection only: failures currently recorded for an account. */\n    public int failureCountFor(String accountSubjectHex) {\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            return live(accounts, accountSubjectHex).failures;\n        }\n    }\n\n    public int failureCountForSource(String sourceHex) {\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            return live(sources, sourceHex).failures;\n        }\n    }\n\n    public boolean isAccountBlocked(String accountSubjectHex) {\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            return live(accounts, accountSubjectHex).blockedUntil > AuditClock.epochSecond();\n        }\n    }\n\n    public boolean isSourceBlocked(String sourceHex) {\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            return live(sources, sourceHex).blockedUntil > AuditClock.epochSecond();\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Seconds until the account back-off lapses; 0 when it is not blocking. */\n    public long accountBlockRemaining(String accountSubjectHex) {\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            long until = live(accounts, accountSubjectHex).blockedUntil;\n            long now = AuditClock.epochSecond();\n            return until > now ? until - now : 0;\n        }\n    }\n\n    public void clearAccountForTesting(String accountSubjectHex) {\n        synchronized (monitor) {\n            accounts.remove(accountSubjectHex);\n            persistLocked();\n        }\n    }\n\n    private State live(Map<String, State> map, String subject) {\n        State s = map.get(subject);\n        long now = AuditClock.epochSecond();\n        if (s == null) {\n            if (map.size() >= MAX_SUBJECTS) {\n                sweep(map);\n                if (map.size() >= MAX_SUBJECTS) {\n                    throw new IllegalStateException(\"throttle-subject-capacity\");\n                }\n            }\n            s = new State();\n            map.put(subject, s);\n        }\n        // Two independent roll-overs, measured against two different reference points.\n        //\n        // (1) The failure *count* window rolls over once it is older than WINDOW_SECONDS\n        // and no block is live. A subject that is currently blocked is never rolled over,\n        // so reading it for a status line cannot erase the count that armed the block.\n        //\n        // (2) The escalation (strikes) decays only after a quiet period measured from the\n        // subject's last event, which for a blocked subject is the moment its block ends --\n        // not the moment it was armed. This distinction is not cosmetic: a 480 s back-off\n        // is longer than WINDOW_SECONDS, so decay measured from the arming time would fire\n        // while the subject is still sitting out its own window. The escalation would then\n        // reset on every round and the back-off could never exceed WINDOW_SECONDS, which\n        // would silently make MAX_ACCOUNT_BLOCK_SECONDS unreachable decoration. Decay still\n        // happens -- after a genuinely quiet period -- and it only ever makes the subject\n        // less restricted, so this direction fails closed.\n        if (s.blockedUntil <= now && now - s.windowStart > WINDOW_SECONDS) {\n            s.windowStart = now;\n            s.failures = 0;\n            s.generation++;\n        }\n        if (now - Math.max(s.windowStart, s.blockedUntil) > WINDOW_SECONDS) {\n            s.strikes = 0;\n        }\n        return s;\n    }\n\n    private void sweep(Map<String, State> map) {\n        long now = AuditClock.epochSecond();\n        map.entrySet().removeIf(e -> now - e.getValue().windowStart > RETENTION_SECONDS\n                && e.getValue().blockedUntil <= now);\n    }\n\n    private void persistLocked() {\n        if (ledger == null) {\n            return;\n        }\n        Map<String, Ledger.Counter> snapshot = new HashMap<>();\n        for (Map.Entry<String, State> e : accounts.entrySet()) {\n            snapshot.put(Ledger.key('a', e.getKey()),\n                    new Ledger.Counter(e.getValue().windowStart, e.getValue().failures,\n                            e.getValue().blockedUntil, e.getValue().generation));\n        }\n        for (Map.Entry<String, State> e : sources.entrySet()) {\n            snapshot.put(Ledger.key('s', e.getKey()),\n                    new Ledger.Counter(e.getValue().windowStart, e.getValue().failures,\n                            e.getValue().blockedUntil, e.getValue().generation));\n        }\n        try {\n            ledger.persist(snapshot);\n        } catch (RuntimeException ledgerDown) {\n            // In-memory enforcement still holds; the decision must not change because\n            // the durability layer is unavailable. Recorded for the operator.\n            ledgerLosses++;\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** A throttle subject must be the 16-hex reference this class documents. */\n    private static String requireRef(String ref) {\n        if (ref == null || ref.length() != Ledger.SUBJECT_HEX || !isLowerHex(ref)) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"throttle-subject-shape\");\n        }\n        return ref;\n    }\n\n    private static boolean isLowerHex(String s) {\n        for (int i = 0; i < s.length(); i++) {\n            char c = s.charAt(i);\n            if (!((c >= '0' && c <= '9') || (c >= 'a' && c <= 'f'))) {\n                return false;\n            }\n        }\n        return true;\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"2658c268-e31a-44d9-8411-56e7e947c69b","session_id":"43a3d1fe-7aa6-43e7-b332-fdd94a07da51","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"2658c268-e31a-44d9-8411-56e7e947c69b","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"I need line numbers. The program does not care which --\n# it loads whatever PKCS12 it is given -- so nothing here has to be redone later.\n#\n# The password is generated, never typed, and never shown:\n#\n#   * it comes from the kernel CSPRNG (/dev/urandom), not from the clock or the PID;\n#   * it is written to a 0600 file, which is the only place it is stored;\n#   * it reaches keytool through the environment (-storepass:env), never as an argument,\n#     because argv is world-readable in /proc while a process's own environment is not;\n#   * it is never echoed. The file is read at most 16 KiB; a larger file is refused rather than truncated.\n#\n# Nothing here is a secret. The keystore password is read from a separate file named by an\n# environment variable, so it never lives in a file that gets copied, backed up or diffed.\n# Do not add a password, a token or a private key to this file: the loader does not know\n# what a secret looks like and will happily echo the KEY back in a startup error.\n#\n# Syntax note (documented, not worked around): this is read with java.util.Properties, so a\n# value loses leading whitespace but keeps trailing whitespace, and `\\` introduces an escape.\n# A host or CIDR written as ` portal.listen.host = 127.0.0.1 ` therefore means the literal\n# `127.0.0.1 `, and the trailing space makes it invalid -- which is refused, not repaired.\n# Write one key per line, no spaces around `=`, no trailing blanks.\n\n# PBKDF2 iterations per password record. Records below the floor refuse to load, so raising\n# this later upgrades new writes without breaking old ones; lowering it below 100000 stops\n# the program from starting at all. Cost is linear in the iteration count: measured on an\n# idle instance of this machine, 100000 is ~165 ms per derivation, 150000 ~215 ms, 500000\n# ~712 ms, 2000000 ~2.85 s. Every login does exactly one derivation, so this number is also\n# the floor on login latency -- raise it with the CPU budget of the host, not past it.\nportal.kdf.iterations = 150000\n\n# Bind address. An IPv6 range such as fd00::/8 is refused at startup\n# (`startup-refused reason=cidr-shape`), not silently ignored -- that refusal is deliberate\n# and worth knowing before you configure\n# an IPv6 load balancer, because the alternative (accepting a range the matcher cannot\n# evaluate) would make per-source throttling spoofable. Everything else in this\n * program is plumbing around it.\n *\n * <h2>The one-digest invariant</h2>\n * Every outcome of {@link #authenticate} executes <b>exactly one</b> PBKDF2 derivation of\n * the presented password at the configured cost, and no more:\n * <ul>\n *   <li>malformed username / malformed password -&gt; one decoy derivation</li>\n *   <li>budget spent -&gt; one decoy derivation (the roster is never read here)</li>\n *   <li>unknown username -&gt; one decoy derivation</li>\n *   <li>known username -&gt; one real verification</li>\n * </ul>\n * CPU work per request is therefore independent of the outcome, and every refusal is\n * additionally padded to a single shared latency floor. \"Account does not exist\",\n * \"wrong password\", \"bad input\" and \"you are rate-limited\" are not distinguishable by\n * timing, and are not distinguishable at all in the response the caller receives.\n *\n * <h2>What a caller may learn</h2>\n * Exactly one bit: success (with a freshly minted session id) or denial.\n * {@link Result#reasonForInternalUseOnly()} exists so the audit log and the test suite can\n * tell branches apart; it is never rendered into a response body, status code, header,\n * cookie, or byte count that a client can observe.\n *\n * <h2>Username folding</h2>\n * {@code ALICE} and {@code alice} are the same employee. The fold is a 26-letter ASCII\n * table applied <em>before</em> validation -- not {@code toLowerCase()} (locale hazard: the\n * Turkish dotted/dotless i) and not Unicode case folding or NFKC (identity hazard:\n * {@code Kelvin} folds onto {@code Kelin}, and a full-width character folds onto a\n * different name). Because the accepted alphabet is {@code [a-z0-9._-]}, this fold is\n * injective over distinct accepted identities: no two real accounts can ever be folded\n * together, so the convenience costs nothing in identity strength.\n */\npublic final class Authenticator {\n\n    /**\n     * Refused-input decoy reference. Not hex, so it can never collide with a real idRef.\n     * Package-visible because {@link AuditLog} recognises it as one of the two non-digest\n     * tokens the subject field accepts.\n     */\n    static final String INVALID_REF = \"invalid\";\n\n    public enum Outcome {\n        SUCCESS, DENIED\n    }\n\n    /** Machine-readable internal reason. Never surfaced to a client. */\n    public enum Reason {\n        CREDENTIALS_OK, BAD_CREDENTIALS, INPUT_INVALID, THROTTLED, KDF_UNAVAILABLE\n    }\n\n    public static final class Result {\n        private final Outcome outcome;\n        private final Reason reason;\n        private final String sessionId;\n        private final String username;\n\n        Result(Outcome outcome, Reason reason, String sessionId, String username) {\n            this.outcome = outcome;\n            this.reason = reason;\n            this.sessionId = sessionId;\n            this.username = username;\n        }\n\n        public boolean isSuccess() {\n            return outcome == Outcome.SUCCESS;\n        }\n\n        public Outcome outcome() {\n            return outcome;\n        }\n\n        /** Non-null only on success. */\n        public String sessionId() {\n            return sessionId;\n        }\n\n        /** Non-null only on success. */\n        public String username() {\n            return username;\n        }\n\n        public String reasonForInternalUseOnly() {\n            return reason.name();\n        }\n    }\n\n    private final EmployeeStore store;\n    private final PasswordHasher hasher;\n    private final ThrottleManager throttle;\n    private final SessionManager sessions;\n    private final AuditLog audit;\n\n    public Authenticator(EmployeeStore store, PasswordHasher hasher, ThrottleManager throttle,\n                         SessionManager sessions, AuditLog audit) {\n        this.store = store;\n        this.hasher = hasher;\n        this.throttle = throttle;\n        this.sessions = sessions;\n        this.audit = audit;\n    }\n\n    /** CLI entry point: the password arrives as the {@code char[]} Console gave us. */\n    public Result authenticate(char[] username, char[] password, String sourceLabel) {\n        char[] folded = username == null ? new char[0] : Authenticator.foldAsciiCase(username);\n        String presented;\n        try {\n            presented = new String(folded);\n        } finally {\n            Crypto.wipe(username);\n            Crypto.wipe(folded);\n        }\n        return authenticate(presented, password, sourceLabel);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * @param presentedUsername attacker-controlled; folded and validated below\n     * @param password          attacker-controlled {@code char[]}; <b>cleared by this\n     *                          method</b> on every path, including success\n     * @param sourceLabel       already-reduced source reference (see {@link SourceRef})\n     */\n    public Result authenticate(String presentedUsername, char[] password, String sourceLabel) {\n        long startedAt = System.nanoTime();\n        try {\n            return decide(presentedUsername, password, sourceLabel, startedAt);\n        } finally {\n            // Single exit point for clearing, so no branch can forget it.\n            Crypto.wipe(password);\n        }\n    }\n\n    private Result decide(String presentedUsername, char[] password, String sourceLabel,\n                          long startedAt) {\n        // Adopt another process's roster change before deciding anything. This is the first\n        // statement of the decision so that the cost is the same on every path -- including\n        // the two malformed-input paths and the throttled path, which never look at a record\n        // and must not be distinguishable by a cost the other paths do not pay. The check is\n        // a stat of the roster file, so it is a function of the file, not of the presented\n        // name, and can therefore never answer \"does this account exist?\".\n        store.refreshIfChanged();\n\n        String sourceHex = SourceRef.normalizeForLedger(sourceLabel);\n        String username = presentedUsername == null ? \"\" : foldAsciiCase(presentedUsername);\n\n        // Each branch below performs exactly one key derivation before calling deny();\n        // that is the invariant stated in the class note, and it is why the derivations\n        // are spelled out at every call site instead of being hidden inside deny().\n        if (!InputPolicy.isUsername(username)) {\n            hasher.burnOneDigest(password, INVALID_REF);\n            throttle.consumeSourceBudget(sourceHex);\n            deny(password, INVALID_REF, sourceHex, Reason.INPUT_INVALID, startedAt);\n            return denial(Reason.INPUT_INVALID);\n        }\n\n        String accountRef = Crypto.idRef(username);\n\n        if (!InputPolicy.isWellFormedSecret(password)) {\n            // Counted against the source budget and padded like every other refusal, so\n            // a malformed password is not a cheap probe of the boundary.\n            hasher.burnOneDigest(password, accountRef);\n            throttle.consumeSourceBudget(sourceHex);\n            deny(password, accountRef, sourceHex, Reason.INPUT_INVALID, startedAt);\n            return denial(Reason.INPUT_INVALID);\n        }\n\n        // The slot is reserved (counter incremented) before the password is looked at, so\n        // a concurrent burst cannot all observe the same headroom and then all verify.\n        ThrottleManager.Kind reserved = throttle.beginAttempt(accountRef, sourceHex);\n        if (reserved == ThrottleManager.Kind.THROTTLED) {\n            // The roster is deliberately not consulted here: reading it would make the\n            // throttle response differ in timing between an existing and an unknown name.\n            // The secret is well-formed at this point, so the real decoy path is safe.\n            hasher.verifyAgainstDecoy(password, accountRef);\n            deny(password, accountRef, sourceHex, Reason.THROTTLED, startedAt);\n            return denial(Reason.THROTTLED);\n        }\n\n        Optional<String> record = store.recordFor(username);\n        if (record.isEmpty()) {\n            hasher.verifyAgainstDecoy(password, accountRef);\n            // Counted exactly like a wrong password, so neither the counter behaviour nor\n            // the cost distinguishes an unknown name from a known one.\n            throttle.recordFailure(accountRef, sourceHex);\n            deny(password, accountRef, sourceHex, Reason.BAD_CREDENTIALS, startedAt);\n            return denial(Reason.BAD_CREDENTIALS);\n        }\n\n        boolean matches;\n        try {\n            matches = hasher.verify(password, record.get(), accountRef);\n        } catch (RuntimeException kdfUnavailable) {\n            // A KDF that cannot run must not read as \"wrong password\" (that would let an\n            // outage lock every account out) nor as \"success\". Fail closed and say so in\n            // the log; the client sees the same generic denial as everybody else.\n            audit.systemFailure(\"login\", accountRef, sourceHex, kdfUnavailable);\n            try {\n                hasher.burnOneDigest(password, accountRef);\n            } catch (RuntimeException kdfStillBroken) {\n                // Nothing left to equalise with; the denial stands either way.\n            }\n            deny(password, accountRef, sourceHex, Reason.KDF_UNAVAILABLE, startedAt);\n            return denial(Reason.KDF_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n\n        if (!matches) {\n            throttle.recordFailure(accountRef, sourceHex);\n            deny(password, accountRef, sourceHex, Reason.BAD_CREDENTIALS, startedAt);\n            return denial(Reason.BAD_CREDENTIALS);\n        }\n\n        // Verified against server-side state. Upgrade the record when the stored KDF cost\n        // is below policy -- reachable only by someone who already knows the password, so\n        // it cannot be used to make the store more expensive for others.\n        if (hasher.needsRehash(record.get())) {\n            try {\n                store.rotateSecret(username, password);\n                audit.event(\"login\", accountRef, sourceHex, \"ok\", \"granted-rotated\");\n            } catch (RuntimeException rotationFailed) {\n                // Authentication succeeded; a failed write is an operational problem and\n                // must not retroactively change the outcome.\n                audit.systemFailure(\"rotate\", accountRef, sourceHex, rotationFailed);\n            }\n        } else {\n            audit.event(\"login\", accountRef, sourceHex, \"ok\", \"granted\");\n        }\n\n        throttle.recordSuccess(accountRef, sourceHex);\n        String session = sessions.issue(username);\n        // Success is padded on the same floor as the refusals, so an observer measuring\n        // latency cannot tell a granted login from a denied one.\n        throttle.padToFloor(startedAt);\n        throttle.observeLatency(System.nanoTime() - startedAt);\n        return new Result(Outcome.SUCCESS, Reason.CREDENTIALS_OK, session, username);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Record the refusal, then pad. Note the order: the audit write is charged\n     * <em>before</em> the padding so the padding covers it, and the padding never decides\n     * anything -- it only equalises.\n     */\n    private void deny(char[] password, String subjectRef, String sourceHex, Reason reason,\n                      long startedAt) {\n        audit.event(\"login\", subjectRef, sourceHex, \"denied\", auditDetail(reason));\n        throttle.padToFloor(startedAt);\n        throttle.observeLatency(System.nanoTime() - startedAt);\n    }\n\n    private static String auditDetail(Reason reason) {\n        return switch (reason) {\n            case BAD_CREDENTIALS -> \"bad-credentials\";\n            case INPUT_INVALID -> \"input-invalid\";\n            case THROTTLED -> \"throttled\";\n            case KDF_UNAVAILABLE -> \"kdf-unavailable\";\n            case CREDENTIALS_OK -> \"granted\";\n        };\n    }\n\n    private static Result denial(Reason reason) {\n        return new Result(Outcome.DENIED, reason, null, null);\n    }\n\n    /** Locale-free ASCII A-Z -&gt; a-z. Touches nothing else. v2|kind:subject|windowStart|count|blockedUntil|generation}. {@code subject} is a\n * SHA-256 reference, never a username or a raw address: a copied or backed-up ledger must\n * not disclose which account is being hammered.\n *\n * <h2>Why merging needs the generation field</h2>\n * Every mutation takes an exclusive {@link FileLock} on a dedicated lock file (which,\n * unlike the data file, is never replaced) and <em>merges</em> with whatever another\n * process persisted, so a racing sibling can never lower our view of the danger -- the\n * merge fails closed. Merging by {@code max(count)} alone, however, is not merely a\n * fail-closed rule: it makes the count <b>monotonic</b>, and the throttle legitimately\n * <em>reduces</em> a count in three places (a correct password, a window rolling over, a\n * failure that arms a back-off and restarts the window). Measured before the generation\n * field existed, six consecutive <em>correct</em> logins of one account denied the sixth:\n * {@link ThrottleManager#recordSuccess} zeroed the counter, persisted {@code count=0}, and\n * the merge immediately restored the {@code count=5} that the previous process had left\n * behind, because 5 &gt; 0 and the merge could not tell a stale window from a live one.\n * Time cannot make that distinction -- a success two seconds after the last failure opens a\n * window that is genuinely new, not a wider interval -- so each subject carries a\n * generation that is bumped exactly when its window is deliberately replaced.\n *\n * <p>Merge rule: entries of the <b>same</b> generation take the maximum count and the\n * maximum blocked-until (the anti-downgrade property a racing sibling must not defeat);\n * entries of <b>different</b> generations take the count of the higher generation, because\n * that writer is describing a window the other has never seen. {@code blockedUntil} is\n * always the maximum: a back-off is never downgraded by a sibling that happens not to know\n * about it.\n *\n * <p>Residual risk, stated plainly: a writer who can already edit this file can invent a\n * high generation and zero a count. That is not a new escape -- the same writer can delete\n * the file outright, which {@link #load} treats as a clean slate -- so the protection here\n * is the file's owner-only mode and directory, not the merge rule.
